648.	“So, if I jacked in, that should trigger a feedback system tick. Normally, I wouldn't care, but it's always there. Anyone halfway competent could check for it. But now, there's nothing. The system logs are exactly the same as when I first checked. It's impossible.”
649.	Having dismissed whatever had caused him to call the halt, Gerhardt had been listening, “So, no system tick means…?” he left the rhetorical sentence hanging.
650.	“Means we’re not here. Someone is going to extreme lengths to hide this place, and now – us.” Lynx felt the chill of his realisation spoken aloud.
651.	Mellie looked to the end of the corridor in which they now stood. More stairs awaited; they were being led upward. Her mind raced. This was supposed to be a no-catch job—Davie's promise. Whatever this was, it contradicted that assurance.
652.	No second-guessing, she mustered her guts to growl out a command, “Garro, take lead. Gerhardt, keep an eye on our rear. Lynx, stick close." Giving a cursory check of her machine-pistol she followed Garro’s advance.
653.	They ascended the stairs in silence, their senses heightened, and their nerves stretched thin. Every step was alive with Lynx’s threat—right now, they didn’t exist. The oppressive silence weighed on them all but could not stop them.
654.	At the top of the stairs, a heavy door stood closed. Garro pushed it open slowly, revealing the rooftop beyond. The cool and still damp night air hit them like a welcome relief after the stifling interior of the building.
655.	The rooftop stretched out before them, an expanse of concrete and shadows. But what caught their attention was not the skyline or the city about them. It was a blacker-than-night outline of a vehicle parked on the roof. They all froze at the sight.
656.	This was, without any doubt, what the co-ordinates were leading to.
 
657.	It took a few moments of apprehensive observation and Gerhardt pointing his pistol at the inanimate vehicle for them to realise there was no grand moment, no explosion, nobody even to greet them or explain. The vehicle—a hover transport and one of a bespoke design, sat across from them—silent, waiting.
658.	“Lynx”, Mellie’s voice tried hard not to leave her throat.
659.	“Uh-huh.” he nodded, pulling his deck from his pack again and approaching the transport. He took a moment to check the exterior, finding what was assumed to be a hardline connection point. He reached out with cable in hand; he did not get to insert the connector, but as his fingers rested on the vehicle’s exterior, it spoke.
660.	《Elliot Alves. Missing, presumed deceased. Registered. I detect three other individuals. Identification required from all participants,》 the synthetic voice announced. It was a female tone, soothing yet rigid, unmistakably artificial.
661.	Lynx spun around, his eyes wide, his jaw agape, looking at the others still by the open door. “I…” he had nothing to further to add to his jaw-gaped, wide-eyed protest.
662.	Mellie cautiously approached the vehicle and touched its side, which was smooth and unnaturally cold. Her touch triggered another announcement: 《Melanie Kaufmann, registered. Two additional individuals detected. Identification required from all participants.》
663.	Gerhardt and Garro shared a look, Garro also glancing pleadingly at Mellie.
664.	“This is why we're here, guys. We're already knee-deep in this—” Mellie gestured to the vehicle, the rest of her sentence fell away into the night.
665.	Taking the cue, Gerhardt stepped forward and, choosing to attempt subterfuge at not giving a biological impression, pressed his cybernetic hand against the vehicle's shell.
666.	《Processing. Gerhardt Kinsley. Three outstanding warrants. Please wait. 》
667.	He snapped back, “Oh, we have to go…” It wasn’t a note of fear or anger but a factual statement. He began a lunging run toward the door from which they had come.
668.	《Gerhardt Kinsley, are these warrants currently a threat to your well-being or an impediment to your immediate tasks? 》The car sounded impatient with the question.
669.	He stumbled at the question, his hand on the doorframe. A moment more, and he would have been inside and heading down the stairs. “What?”
670.	《My apologies. Let me re-phrase: are the warrants against you likely to be enforced in the near future, or are you currently uninhibited to take on a specialist role? 》
671.	Turning back to face the enigmatic vehicle, he stepped backwards through the doorway. For the first time, his voice trembled, a subtle break in his usually stoic demeanour. “Are you accessing Cit-Sec? Or the Keepers?”
672.	《My first priority is to ensure the safety of this operation. I must know if your circumstances pose a threat.》
673.	“Only three huh? And… You don’t care? – No, no threat from me.” His voice once again recovered to an indecipherably mundane average tone and timbre.
674.	《Gerhardt Kinsley, registered. One remaining individual detected. Identification required from all participants.》
675.	Garro patted Gerhardt on the shoulder, gave a pursed smile and took two giant strides to the vehicle, letting his hand slap against the hull of the thing.
676.	《Special Field Sergeant Piotr Garonosovietch, discharged. Registered. No other individuals detected in close proximity. Closing registration.》The voice hadn’t even finished reverberating in the night air before a panel in the side of the vehicle hissed in protest of an atmospheric seal releasing.
677.	The panel split, and the halves slid apart to reveal a luxurious cabin. Seats upholstered in what appeared to be genuine leather beckoned them. If not for the drizzle reminding them of their surroundings, they might have thought they'd stumbled upon a plush apartment encased in metal—waiting just for them.
678.	Garro, having now accepted that the worst that can happen is a sudden and horrifying death, eagerly hops in, slumping into one of the chairs. “Well, this isn’t the worst thing to happen. May as well do whatever it is we’re doin’.”
679.	Lynx climbs in after Garro, and Mellie—also resigned to their uncertain course of action—holsters her pistol and steps inside. Gripping a grab-bar overhead, she waves at Gerhardt with her free hand. “All aboard the fuck-knows express.”
680.	Taking one last look around the desolate rooftop, Gerhardt reaches for Mellie's outstretched hand and pulls himself into the transport.
681.	The vehicle makes no noise as it softly ascends into the night above Akureyri, its occupants stiff with tension and anxiety, sharing confused looks. The engines hum delicately to full power, and the impossibly dark outline of the transport melds into the sky, disappearing from view.
682.	Below, the city slides by—neon and alive, shadowed and sleeping. Ahead, rising from the distant horizon of buildings, an enormous glass tower looms, its surface glittering with the reflected lights of the city.
Chapter Eleven:	Taken For a Ride.
683.	The group sat silently, enveloped by the nearly inaudible hum of the vehicle's engine. Garro leaned forward to open what appeared to be a mini-bar, only to grumble a complaint about its emptiness as he settled, creaking back into his leather seat.
684.	Abruptly, screens at the front and rear of the cabin and one above the mini-bar blinked to life. Initially black, the screens soon displayed a message in calm-blue font: [A short message will now follow.]
685.	A distorted and layered computerized voice began to speak from speakers throughout the cabin. The words were difficult to understand due to the unnatural tone, yet the voice conveyed an unnerving sense of apathy.
686.	《Thank you for entertaining my request,》 it said.
687.	《Fulfilment of this task will carry with it a reward of two point five million D.C.R. The value is set to reflect the severity and importance of this task.》
688.	Ever the pragmatist, Gerhardt nodded in acknowledgement, already weighing expectant risks that might garner such a  reward in his mind. The sum was far more than Davie had promised, making the mission suddenly more meaningful to him and likely – a great deal more dangerous to them all.
689.	Lynx’s eyes were wide, and his jaw hung loosely open, stunned by the revelation.

690.	《A great injustice has been done to me,》the computerized voice resumed, its tone as dispassionate as ever. 《I task you with reconciling this matter outside of normal law enforcement channels.》
691.	《You are currently being transported to a location that must remain absolutely secret,》the voice paused, perhaps intentionally. Its unchanging tone somehow emphasized the unspoken threat in the words.《While in my employ, your safety is paramount. You will be afforded the same securities I grant myself.》
692.	Mellie's hand trembled slightly as she grasped her armrest. A promise of safety was reassuring, but it came from a faceless corporate employer. Her gut raged in resentment of the trap she sat now sat in.
693.	《Your task is simple: Find the person or persons responsible. Confirm their identity and involvement in this matter. Then, execute them summarily.》
694.	Lynx, still reeling from the announcement of the monumental amount of money being offered, now sat in shock. He exchanged a glance with the others. "Execute them?" he mumbled, his voice trembling.
695.	The uncaring voice continued, 《Upon completion of the task, you will provide me with details of the perpetrators' identities, their locations at the time of execution, and information about their frequented locales, all via secure datalink.》
696.	《Should you supply details I determine to be of interest, you may receive an additional bonus payment.》
697.	The group exchanged bewildered glances. Their faces were pale, the gravity of the situation fully dawning on them. This was no mere cash grab; they were entangled in dangerous corporate machinations. It felt as if Davie had signed their death warrants.
698.	《Your journey is soon to end. 》 the voice intoned. 《Upon arrival, you will be greeted by my personal security liaison. You will have one hour to survey the crime scene and discuss the matter with them.》
699.	As the message continued, Garro looked out the darkened window; his thoughts ploughed through murk as he pondered what this message was leading to.
700.	《You will then depart via this same hover transport and be taken to a secluded disembarkation location,》the voice continued, its tone unchanged—but its words resounding with increasing gravity.《The following is critically important: Do not divulge or recall any details about the location or its contents to anyone.》
701.	《Your involvement in this matter is extraneous to Cit-Sec operations concerning this matter. Do not interfere with their investigations.》
702.	《If you fail to gather the required details, or should you fail in your task of executing the perpetrator, you shall be paid an exiguous remittance of ten thousand DCR for your time and your ongoing and continued nondisclosure about this operation. You will be released from my service without consequence.》
703.	《If you succeed in achieving an outcome that meets the details of this brief, you will leave my service with my gratitude and without consequence brought about by myself or those in my employ.》The voice paused, as if to catch its breath—if a machine could do so.
704.	《Should it become apparent that you have betrayed my trust, a bounty shall be placed upon you for your execution, and I shall enforce that Cit-Sec is to consider you to be a kill-on-sight target. Your agreed participation cannot be breached.》
705.	Mellie's fingers dug into the irreplaceable leather. The consequences of failure or betrayal of a corporation were unequivocal—absolute, even. The threat conjured haunting echoes of her father's fate.
706.	《If you decide not to engage in this operation, activate the datapad in your cabin. You will trigger a fail-safe reroute and  be delivered to a predetermined location without consequence.》A quiet pop sounded as a colourful panel revealed itself from behind a sliding cover.
707.	Mellie immediately thrust herself across the cabin toward it, but Gerhart’s cybernetic arm snaked out, snaring her wrist faster than humanly possible.
708.	“LET, FUCKING, GO!” Mellie roared. Her face streamed with tears, her eyes aflame with fury.
709.	“Mellie—” Gerhardt said, his voice nearly as devoid of emotion as the car's mechanical voice, “I need this. We need this.”
710.	Mellie started beating on his chest with her free hand; he turned his head and allowed the assault—all the while, his mechanical arm, unyielding and implacable, kept Mellie from reaching the activation panel.
711.	Through the fury that Mellie was exploding, Lynx leaned forward, his voice strained but conciliatory, “Mel, let's consider this. Two and a half million– that's a game-changer. We can't just ditch it.”
712.	“Are you insane?” Mellie's eyes locked onto Lynx; her fiery gaze slightly dimmed but still intense. “This is a death sentence! A trap! We didn't sign up for this!”
713.	Lynx shrank back, making himself small. Mellie couldn’t move, but the cabin was small enough that her complete and consuming rage flooded it from end to end. Quietly and defensively, Lynx shielded himself, “Maybe Davie didn't know.”
714.	Garro, stretching his arm across Gerhardt, took hold of both Gerhardt's hand and Mellie's arm. “Let her go.” It was an instruction that was soft, but each word tensed by every muscle he had.
715.	Gerhardt looked between Mellie and Garro, an unspoken conversation that was easily understood. Mellie felt the cold fingers unwrap from her wrist and recede, but Garro’s giant paw remained.
716.	Garro spoke in a voice both gentle and authoritative, "Maybe it’s not all bad, Mel. Think about it. We've been scraping by in this cesspool of a city. Maybe it’s time we took a chance to get some for ourselves?"
717.	Mellie turned her fire and incredulity toward Garro, her anger redirected. "You can't be serious, Gee. We don't even know who this voice belongs to. This could be some psycho Corp exec with a vendetta."
718.	Garro shrugged, his nonchalance seemingly unshakable, "We don't. But we can be smart about it. Get the info first, check it out, then decide." His lucidity was shocking.
719.	Still seething but reluctantly listening, Mellie asked the most pressing question, "How do we know they won't double-cross us? Promises mean nothing to these fucks."
720.	Garro allowed her to sit back down and let her arm go. She rubbed at her wrist where clenched metallic fingers had left pressure imprints.
721.	The cabin was silent; Mellie's eyes once more looked to the panel and then around the others. “Screw it, I guess we’re all fucking mad.” she finally conceded, rolling her eyes and accepting an unwelcome truth.
722.	No one spoke as the vehicle began to slow down, almost imperceptibly. Outside, the flickering of landing lights began to cast soft, coloured shadows into the cabin.
723.	They had arrived.
Chapter Twelve:	Fool Me Once.
724.	The cracking of the panel doors on the vehicle hissed gently as they slid open. Gerhart waited, his pistol already drawn and laid across his lap—facing the opening doors, a hand hovering in anticipation.
725.	The scene outside was bright – neon and fluorescent lights washed out the entirety of the landing pad the hover transport now rested on and drew a haloed silhouette of a slender, feminine figure standing just beyond the doors. “Good evening, please—“ a polite voice intoned, the words precise and airy. “There are some formalities to attend to before we proceed.”
726.	They each disembarked and took in the surroundings, each of them in their own time being dazzled and left in awe at the scene about them.
727.	The landing area was perched near the top of an impossibly tall building, and the wind whipped and flurried about them, tugging at Mellie's long coat. Looking outward, most of the city was below them, and only a few other towers dared reach as high.
728.	Across the expansive landing area lay a jumble of containers and a makeshift metal structure—essentially an improvised room where another vehicle might have parked. A throng of uniformed men and women milled about, all bearing the insignia of City Security—Cit-Sec.
729.	Gerhardt's eyes scanned the crowd, meticulously noting each individual and the several bipedal support droids. Seeing the Cit-Sec insignia on their uniforms, he felt a prickling unease. 
730.	It was a small army of personnel, enough to handle a serious operation. Given his outlaw status and warrants, the presence of Cit-Sec made him feel like a wolf surrounded by hunters. He couldn't shake the nagging itch at the back of his mind that wondered what they were all doing here—and what it meant for him.
731.	As the landing lights dimmed, the person addressing them stepped forward, “I will be your liaison. I understand you have already received instructions?” Her voice was slight enough that the air currents that eddied against the building nearly hid her words.
732.	Mellie took the lead, gesturing to the small group. “Yeah, we got the brief. But who exactly are you, and what's happening here?”
733.	“My apologies.” The liaison moved forward, her tight black dress with thin red accents and the impeccable lines of very expensive and high-tech aesthetic implants giving the impression of a mannequin with a kind accent. “There are certain things I cannot divulge; you are here for one purpose only and—”
734.	“This them?” an untidy male voice cut her off; approaching from the Cit-Sec camp, a man with a cleanly shaven head adorned in a full tactical armour suit was striding toward them.
735.	With a military-like snap of attention, the liaison turned to the newcomer. “Captain, as I've already informed you, you are not to interfere with our specialists. I suggest you return to your situation room and await further instructions.” Her voice was commanding but remained soft.
736.	Lynx glanced at Gerhardt and shrugged. Seeing someone talk to Cit-Sec in such a manner was unsettling. In his eyes, whoever had the authority to command Cit-Sec was now their employer. It was not an easy feeling.
737.	Pulling her already perfectly formed dress into place, the liaison turned again—back to the group—and calmly pulled back strands of onyx-dark hair that had spilt across her face. Gestures that also seemed to dismiss the Cit-Sec captain, who now stood impotent in a conversation he had been ejected from.
738.	“My apologies for the interruption,” she said, returning attention to the group. “City Security is quite adamant about having priority, but my employer has already declared that they are not to have access before we can make an independent assessment. And that is why you are here. Please, follow me.” With a graceful pirouette, she led them toward the building's entryway.
739.	The Cit-Sec captain glared at them as they followed the liaison. Gerhardt met his gaze squarely, his thoughts distracted. Why are they here, and acting like lapdogs? With trepidation, he picked up the heavy bag containing his rifle and joined the others.
740.	“You will have exactly one hour to survey the area”, the liaison continued as they walked. “I can answer any questions you may have about the area – within reason. Your focus should remain solely on the area I will guide you to.”
741.	As they approached, enormous security doors silently parted, revealing a pristine granite hallway. On either side of the doors, armed Cit-Sec droids watched them intently. Garro flinched a grimace at the sight; his near-fatal encounter with a similar droid was still a fresh memory.
742.	“Regrettably late, it has come to our attention that there was a catastrophic failure in the internal monitoring systems,” the liaison added, her voice tinged with a hint of urgency.
743.	The massive doors sealed shut behind them as they proceeded down the hallway, cutting off any ambient noise from outside. All that remained was the liaison's soft voice and the rhythmic click of her heels. “Your primary concern will be that an item has been stolen- A data source. You are to survey the area, make necessary investigations and follow your instructions.”
744.	With a graceful push of her near-porcelain arms, the liaison opened another set of double doors, leading them into the grand lobby of the penthouse.
 
745.	Lynx stumbled slightly.
746.	Mellie felt her stomach drop as she saw the mangled remains of a simple defence droid up ahead.
747.	Garro opens his mouth, “Ah, we—” A razor glance from Mellie stops him.
748.	Gerhardt froze, his cybernetic eye flicking over the liaison as if scanning her for concealed threats or clues. A chilling realization began to unfold in his mind.
749.	“So, if you have no immediate questions, I would advise that you begin your investigation promptly. You will find the data centre toward the rear.” The liaison stood expectantly, her arm extending an elegant gesture of her instruction.
750.	The apartment was hauntingly familiar; it was exactly as they had left it. The missing skylight above allowed the night air to seep in, and the stars far above seemed to stare down in silent accusation. The dark room felt like a claw about to crush them, a trap they’d set unwittingly for themselves.
751.	“We’ll need to survey the area properly.” His cybernetic eye picked up the subtle, almost invisible signs of tension in his companions: quickened pulses, beads of sweat forming despite the cold air, taut muscles primed; they were all thinking the same thing but were too afraid to say it.
752.	Mellie took a cautious step forward; her voice tinged with disbelief, “And… You say your monitor systems failed completely?”
753.	The liaison's smile was polite but empty, a humanoid embodiment of an encyclopaedia, “Yes, the exact cause has yet to be determined. Another specialist team is investigating that. You need not concern yourselves.”
754.	Lynx felt the moment and stepped into the conversation, “What about local grid? Surely that thing had to be running on something?” he pointed at the corpse of the droid.
755.	The liaison turned toward Lynx with fluid yet mechanical precision. “According to our internal logs, there was not only a fault in the building's network systems but also internal tampering.” Her eyes flitted to the pack Lynx held tightly, the corner of his deck protruding slightly, “Mr. Alves, I believe you're more qualified than I am to delve into the specifics of such matters.” Her smile was polite, her demeanour patient, but her words carried an unspoken edge that silenced Lynx.
756.	“Right,” Lynx responded, deliberately pointing in the wrong direction. “And you said the data centre is at the rear?”
757.	Garro had already wandered off into the penthouse, happily flicking through cupboards in the kitchen, the sound of which prompted Mellie to try and shift the dialogue.
758.	“We have an hour for our investigation, right? Maybe it's best if you wait outside. We have our own methods, a little different from Cit-Sec. We'll need some space. Oh, and make sure those badges don't get in the way.”
759.	The liaison took a moment before responding with another impeccably polite smile.
760.	“Certainly. But, you are not to leave this apartment.” The statement was absolute and given with a tone of authority, immediately switching into a receptionist's acquiescence, “If you have any further questions, I shall await you outside.” With graceful, dancer-like movements, she retreated to the double doors and closed them softly behind her, plunging the penthouse into near-silence except for Garro's distant rummaging.
761.	“What the hell?” Gerhardt sputtered into the quiet, his face drawn with a very uncharacteristically clear portrayal of confusion.
762.	“They have no clue…” Mellie mumbled, seemingly to herself rather than the group.
763.	From the hallway, Garro yelled to them, “Guys, what are we supposed to do? We already—”
764.	A collective, aggressive “Shush!” reverberated from the group.
765.	Mellie paced in a small circle, lost in thought, while the others watched her. Garro, distracted by some dried snacks in the kitchen, resumed his exploration.
766.	Thumbing at her lower lip, Mellie struts for a brief moment. “Lynx, can you check that the security systems are functional?” Mellie had paused her pacing and stared with some intensity at Lynx, seeming to choose each word carefully—as if trying to communicate something of great importance to him telepathically.
767.	“Check… The security?” Lynx echoed, momentarily puzzled. Under Mellie's intense gaze, a light seemed to go on. “Ah, check the security. Got it.” He nodded emphatically, confirming Mellie's hidden instructions, and hurried to an access port. Fumbling slightly, he pulled a tangle of wires from his pack.
768.	Mellie turned her attention to Gerhardt. “Once Lynx confirms the systems are secure, we can start investigating.”
769.	“Mm, yeah”, Gerhardt responded curtly. Whether he was playing along with Mellie's silent cues or reverting to his typical stoic demeanour was unclear. Indifferent to whichever notion he might carry, the penthouse atmosphere squeezed tightly as Lynx worked.
770.	“Systems are all off. But there’s definitely someone poking about somewhere.” Lynx announced after a moment.
771.	“Fuck”, Mellie exhaled, releasing tension.
772.	“Are we good? No signs? – blackout – ghost, whatever you jack-jockeys call it?” Gerhardt's voice had a sharper edge than usual.
773.	“Yes, but I’ll run over a full sweep for anything unusual. Even that bot’s logs n’ see if we’ve got anything left in here.” Lynx replied, diving back into his work. His hands worked fervently at his deck, rhythmic, precise movements of someone in their element—he was a swimmer in a pool, a runner on a track.
774.	While Lynx continued his work, the others had left to retrace their steps around the penthouse. Garro took great pleasure in revisiting the plush fittings and fixtures, enjoying a brief lay-down on a bed that likely cost more than most people's homes.
775.	Mellie and Gerhardt, on the other hand, were engrossed in a whispered, animated conversation, trying to figure out how to best erase any evidence of their previous visit. The urgency of their situation seemed to animate Gerhardt in a way Mellie had never seen before.
776.	Mellie ended their futile, hushed discussion and headed to Garro. Pulling him to face her and holding both shoulders, she locked eyes with him. He returned a confused half-smile.
777.	“Hey, tell me you know what is going on here.” Mellies’s voice was tight.
778.	“Sure, those suits want us to find somethin’, but we already came here before and—” Garro's words were cut off as Mellie gave him a gentle shake.
779.	“No, those people want us dead. Do you remember the message in the car?” Mellie's grip on his shoulders loosened as she realized she was holding him a bit too tightly.
780.	“Yeah, but the message said we should be hunting people.” Confusion wavered in his deep voice. “Seems like a good job—” he trailed off as Mellie shook her head, gripping her forehead with one hand in exasperation.
781.	“No Gee, they are confused. They don't know it was us who broke in here. And if they find out, we're fucked.” Mellie said, her face stern. She saw the realization dawn on Garro's face, who began nodding earnestly. “So, We’re going to check the place out to see if we can hide what happened, take out any trace we were ever here, and we are going to get out all smooth like. You with me?”
782.	Lifting his hand in a lazy salute, Garro nodded, his face now set with what passed for determination. Mellie motioned for him to follow her.
783.	With Garro's assistance, Mellie climbed out of the skylight onto the rooftop where she had previously lain in overwatch. She scoured the area for any leftover bullet casings or any other trace of their previous presence.
784.	In all the fuss, Gerhardt, with liberal excessive use of his cyber-eye’s magnification and polarizing filters, had become a sniffer dog - hunting for damning evidence outside of normal vision. While he knew that a Cit-Sec forensic team would find more than he could, he had to at least try. Sweeping surfaces, checking for accusing fingerprints - it was a common part of a job to tidy up, but now he was paying extra attention to even the most minor details.
785.	Each of them, even Garro, could sense that just beyond the grand entry doors lay enough firepower to make for a disastrous evening if things went sideways —they needed to ensure that traces of their past presence were thoroughly eradicated. It wasn't just about making the place look 'right'; it had to look 'right' in a way that completely excluded them.
 
786.	An hour wasn’t nearly enough time, Mellie thought as the liaison gracefully pushed open the double doors to re-enter the penthouse.
787.	Lynx was still jacked in, buckled over his deck; Garro squatted next to the fragmented robot toyed with the now defunct taser appendage, and Gerhardt had only just appeared from the deeper shadows striding back into the open plan hub of the penthouse.
788.	“I apologize for the interruption,” the liaison announced, her voice devoid of actual regret. “Time is up for your survey. City Security forces must now be given access per our arranged protocol. I trust you have been productive and have developed an actionable plan? I must report your activity to my superior.” It was with a soothing politeness she gave the insistent declaration. An authority that defied contradiction yet delivered with pristine cultivated grace.
789.	Mellie pulled her feigned attention from the destroyed security droid, a final glance at Garroto to assess his place in the pantomime they shared. "Yes, I think we are done here– Lynx?”
790.	Lynx hastily packed away his deck and looked up, signalling 'okay' with a thumb and forefinger. “Yeah, I am positive that we’re done here”, he emphasized, ensuring Mellie caught his meaning.
791.	Gerhardt fell in step with Mellie as they collectively made their way toward the exit. “You can tell your boss we already have a good idea who to look for, and we aren’t going to waste any time. One thing though, the payment, how can we be sure we’ll get a dime once we wrap this?”
792.	The liaison spoke calmly but with a scent of tedium at such a banal question: “The funds will be placed in a secure escrow account. You can, at any time, check the availability of the remuneration. Upon successful completion of your task, you'll be provided with a release code to access the account. Rest assured, the details will be forwarded to you.” Her words, mellifluous and yet tinged with finality, left no room for further inquiry.
793.	Garro appeared from the opposite side of the open-plan lounge area, a bag of snacks in one hand, the other happily tossing a few in his mouth. Oblivious to the tension, he greeted the impassive liaison with a courteous nod as he caught up to the others.
794.	As the quartet make their way back along the granite corridor, they are greeted by the faceless head-to-toe suits of the Cit-Sec forensics specialists coming in and moving toward the penthouse, boxes of gear and instruments being brought in. The captain they'd met earlier was among them, engrossed in a conversation on a datapad with another officer.
795.	Gerhardt passed by the Cit-Sec team, paused for a moment, and then turned to say, “Good luck. You’ll not find anything.” Though his comment was met with indifference, he couldn't help but flash a small, self-satisfied smile as he resumed his casual stroll toward the exit.
796.	The hov-trans was waiting for them as they emerged from the building, and they boarded in silence, each lost in their own thoughts. The liaison, all smiles and gracious gestures, spoke as they entered, but her words seemed to fade into irrelevance.
797.	No words were shared, and no glances were exchanged—The journey away from the tower was a void.
798.	Aside from the soft hum of the engine, Gerhardt's rhythmic tapping of his carbon-fibre fingertips against the polished teak wood was the only sound.
799.	The transport landed not far from the building they had found it parked on, separated by what felt like the eternity of the events of the night.
800.	Mellie watched as the hov-trans lifted away, its dark silhouette receding into the ever-present glow of the restless city, barely visible against the lightening morning sky.
801.	For a long moment, the ambient noise of the city—engines, sirens, and distant advertisements—filled the silence as they grappled with their thoughts. Finally, Mellie broke the quiet.
802.	"Davie.”
803.	It was a single damning word, but they all knew what was meant by it.
Chapter Thirteen:	Better the Devil You Know.
804.	The auto-cab had picked up Lynx, Mellie, and Garro and was doing its best to navigate the morning traffic. Inside, crammed against Garro’s barrel-chest, the crew were a jumble of discussion.
805.	“We should find out what the fuck is going on”, Lynx’s small voice protruded.
806.	“Can we get breakfast?” Garro shuffled his weight a little so that Lynx’s deck wasn’t poking his ribs.
807.	“We should be making plans to get out of town.” Mellie’s grumbles were layered and lost within the shared conversation.
808.	While no one seemed to be genuinely conversing, they all shared the sentiment that something needed to be done.
809.	Having decided to make his own way and retreating to find his bike for travel, Gerhardt was elsewhere. His agenda didn’t immediately align –he needed to secure his rifle back at his apartment. The last thing he needed now was to get pulled over with such a firearm in public. Displaced from the conversation in the back of the cab, he joined unknowingly, thinking– I’m going to wring his scrawny neck, as he weaved expertly through the chaos of commuters, pulling a roar from the engine to thrust him toward sanctuary.
810.	The cab pulled up outside a small diner on the edge of the Skrim. Garro's stomach audibly grumbled as he caught the scent of fresh breakfast tacos. While the three in the cab continued to argue about their next moves, Garro headed into the diner. He ordered a large platter and sat down, eagerly awaiting the arrival of 'neat-meat'; whatever that was, it smelled good.
811.	As if pulled by Garro's gravitational force, Mellie and Lynx followed him inside. Their fruitless discussion persisted as cups of brown liquid were poured and consumed. Amid the clatter of dishes and chatter of early-morning patrons, Garro happily dug into a grease-dripping taco.
812.	Lynx, tapping at his wrist bracer, furrowed his brow. “I’ve tried getting hold of Davie, but he’s not picking up. I sent a com to him, it just says recipient unavailable.”
 -=X=-
813.	Screeching through the northern edge of the Skrim, heading quickly to the expressway, Gerki was already deep in a conversation over his embedded agent. “I told you, Cit-Sec were there in force. No way we could have pulled out. This is on you.”
814.	Davie’s voice cracked as he responded, “Hold on, I didn't know—”
815.	“Didn't know? Or didn't care? It doesn’t matter. We’re sitting ducks now. We’ve got to move fast before they come down on us.”
816.	“Look, I feel for you, I do. But right now, It’s probably best we just lay low.”
817.	“Easy for you to say. You've got resources, connections. What am I supposed to do?” A van caused Gerhart to swerve sharply as he tersely responded inside of his helmet.
818.	“You're resourceful, Gerki. You'll figure it out. If what you say about Cit-Sec is true, I need to dodge this one before it comes at me.”
819.	“So that's it? You're just going to abandon me to clean up your mess?”
820.	“It's not about abandoning anyone. But I can't fix this for you. We're all in deep. Look out for yourself, Gerki. That's the game.”
821.	“Damn you, Davie. If I go down for this...”
822.	“You won't. Just keep your head down. I've got to sort my own shit out. This is on all of us.” The line went silent; Davie had cut the connection.
 -=X=-
823.	Taking a deep breath to contain her simmering irritation, Mellie spoke. "Okay, we need answers, and we need them now. Davie's Club – It's our best shot at finding him. He's usually there during opening hours. So, we wait till later, find him, and shake him till the info falls out."
824.	Garro nodded along, ramming another taco in his mouth.
825.	Exchanging glances, they realized they had reached a rare consensus. Confronting Davie was their only option. The fact that they had to wait until the club opened was a minor annoyance, especially when they considered that Cit-Sec was likely drafting kill-on-sight warrants for them at that very moment.
826.	Lynx was the first to leave – “I’m gonna keep trying to get Davie on comms. He can’t ditch me like this.” His eyes were puffy and tired, but he carried an energy that only youth and a belly full of processed protein could provide.
827.	Mellie paid up for their morning stopover and let Garro take a bag of scraps with him as they walked the remaining journey through the lower belly of the Skrim, headed to Mellies’s apartment for a rest before facing whatever awaited them later.
828.	Time had caught up with them, and even the harsh chemicals masquerading as beverages weren’t enough. Sleep was needed.
Chapter Fourteen:	Shaking the Sleeper's Throne
829.	Hours passed, and the city outside transformed from the bustling chaos of the morning to the subdued hum of mid-afternoon. The daily lives of millions of people ticking their tocks, unchanged and unknowing of the complexities that surrounded the four most pivotal people among them.
830.	Lynx stirred from a restless slumber in the dim light of an abandoned parking garage. This once-busy hub, now a cavern of echoes and stillness, cradled his makeshift bed among its concrete expanses. Faint sunlight, filtered through years of grime on the windows, cast a gentle, diffused light on the scene — his backpack, a few personal items, and the deck lay scattered around him.
831.	Lynx had fallen asleep in a pile of trash, awakening to a smell that made him wretch slightly. He checked the time; it had just passed four in the afternoon. Pulling the deck from his pack, he flicked through the interface. There was no sign of any messages, and the system logs reported ‘recipient unavailable’.
832.	He thought to himself, There’s nobody in this city that’s unavailable. The only time anyone is unavailable is when they’ve taken up residence on some meat slab with a toe tag.
833.	Switching gears, he tried to reach Gerhardt. Though he could see the connection details on his deck's screen, it was in his mind that he heard the soft trilling of the connection.
834.	“Yes?” Gerhardt’s curt introduction cut straight to the point, Ignoring all pleasantries and aiming for the jugular of the conversation.
835.	“Uhh, Hi, had you spoken with Davie? I tried earlier but got nothing. Honestly – it’s weird, man. He’s just… gone.” Lynx replied as he picked himself up from the pile of trash and began slowly walking down a desolate alleyway.
836.	A sigh came from Gerhardt, tinged with a shared frustration. “Seems Davie's gone off the deep end, making his own plays. It's like we’re pawns who've just found out the board's on fire.”
837.	“Yeah, feels like we're all scrambling in the dark here. The others and I were thinking of seeing him at the club,” Lynx replied, his voice reflecting a weariness that mirrored Gerhardt's.
838.	“Was going to do the same myself. Need to corner him before he slips out, get some straight answers.” Gerhardt responded, his tone resolute yet weary from the unfolding chaos.
839.	“Agreed. I’m already headed there, meet ya there?” 
840.	“The club opens in an hour though. I’m going to freshen up – I’ll be on the plaza in about thirty minutes.” Gerhardt's voice dragged a little. 
841.	“Cool, I’ll let the other know”, Lynx culled the connection. He pondered from the sound of Gerhard's voice whether he had slept or not, but Lynx had no time to concern himself with the intrigue of that curiosity; he mentally summoned a new set of information and began a new connection. 
 
842.	The afternoon heat mingled with a growing sense of disarray within the apartment. Mellie, her bronze hair slightly dishevelled, sat cramped at the edge of crumpled bedding. The room was warm and hazy, mirroring the city's steady hum outside. On the worn-out couch, Garro slept, oblivious to the flickering vid-screen across from him. Mellie blearily pressed the ‘accept call’ button, and Lynx's face materialized on a holo-screen.
843.	“I guess it’s time to get going?” she asked with the reticence of someone only half awake. “Hey, Garro”, she called out, grabbing and tossing something at his slumbering, dishevelled form amidst the clutter.
844.	The boot that was flung hit him square in the guts; the battered sofa on which he lay took the aromatic brunt of the breakfast tacos as he stirred.
845.	“Aww man, fuck, that’s bad”, Mellie protested, wincing.
846.	“What?” Lynx's glowing, projected face looked confused.
847.	“Not you,” Mellie said, turning her attention back to the holo-screen and waving her hand in front of her nose as if it were a fan. “Yeah, we’ll be there just as soon as I can get that stink off of my couch.”
848.	“Gerki’s coming too. I just spoke with him. I—”
849.	“Eh, whatever.” Mellie pressed a button, cutting Lynx’s words.
850.	Garro abruptly sat up with a groan, feeling the impact where the boot had hit. "What's that for?" he mumbled, still half in a daze.
851.	"Time to move," Mellie retorted, her tone sharp as she swiftly pulled on a pair of trousers and gathered her things. She moved with a brisk efficiency, urgency replacing lethargy.
852.	Garro, rubbing his stomach, swung his legs off the couch and rose to his feet. The club awaited.
 
853.	The Halcyon Club would be opening soon, and the crew began to reassemble on the bustling plaza just a short walk from their destination. Neon signs from surrounding businesses jostled for attention, bathing the area in a kaleidoscope of shifting colours and patterns. The scent of street food wafted through the air, mixing with the low hum of distant conversations. Somewhere down an unseen street, a car horn blared as if two locals were having a public argument via their vehicles' horns, loud enough for everyone within half a kilometre to hear.
854.	Lynx arrived first. Even in his stained and dishevelled state, he didn't look out of place here. The other denizens of the Skrim—garishly coloured, draped in fake leather or clad in work clothes—milled about. He rechecked his wrist bracer, half-expecting to find a message from Davie. The screen remained blank, intensifying his frustration as he scanned the plaza for his friends.
855.	Garro appeared next, an unmistakable figure drawing occasional curious glances from passersby. He approached Lynx with a nod, and they exchanged a few words in hushed tones, their voices lost amidst the surrounding chatter.
856.	Mellie followed closely behind him, her lithe frame weaving through the crowd with ease. Despite the exhaustion that clung to her like a second skin, she exuded determination. She greeted Lynx with an upward nod and raised eyebrows, her lips slightly pursed.
857.	Not far away, Gerhardt was birthed from the shadows. His movements were graceful and precise, his face an inscrutable mask, but his human eye betrayed a sense of urgency. He joined the group, completing the circle.
858.	Beyond a bout of courteous acknowledgements, no one spoke. In offset quiet unison, they turned to leave. An uncomfortable silence marked the short journey to the Halcyon. A sense of uncertainty hovered around them. Gerhardt, in particular, seemed to simmer with an anxiety that, while outwardly calm, felt dangerously close to erupting.
 
859.	The bass beat of the club hammered into the street; its low rumbles were felt echoing underfoot as they approached. The visually twinned doormen, almost as imposing as Garro, nodded in recognition.
860.	“Davie in?” Mellie Cawed over the noise.
861.	“Dave’s not here.” responded the taller, darker-skinned of the two bouncers.
862.	“What d’you mean?” Mellie's voice strained against the thumping music and the surrounding crowd. “You know what, never mind. We’ll find him.”
863.	Mellie waved at the others and let her long stride carry her into the club. As they entered, they all began craning their necks; the VIP lounge was a good spot to check, and if not there, then the office was their next option.
864.	There were no immediate signs of Davie.
865.	Gerhardt approached the bar, where a silver-skinned, blue-haired waitress greeted him with a bright smile. “What can I get you?”
866.	“Where’s Davie?”
867.	The waitress's brow furrowed, her previously welcoming smile faltering into a tight line as she stared at Gerhardt. It was as though his words had traversed the boundaries of language, leaving her to grasp at meaning in a sea of unfamiliar sounds. “Who?” she echoed, the brightness in her eyes dimming with her smile.
868.	“Davie,” he reiterated, pointing at the office windows above. The lights danced and glinted on his augmented arm.
869.	The waitress shrugged and offered an apologetic look of confused apathy, backed up by half-smiling eyes and a grin that would make many men want to give overly generous tips.
870.	Returning to the group, Gerhardt shared his lack of success. The others had also come up empty in their search.
871.	Yelling to be heard over the music, Gerhardt turned to Lynx. “If he’s not here, the office will be locked. But if there is anything he knows - It’s up there. Think you can crack his system?”
872.	Lynx's eyes widened as he let the question vibrate among the bass. “I mean, I could, but... It’s Davie, man. If Stepper gets a sniff I’ve been shitting on my own doorstep. I may as well hand myself to Cit-Sec.”
873.	Gerhardt nods sharply. “So you can do it. That's all I need to know. I'll get us upstairs; you just suck out whatever data the weasel has. Let me handle the rest,” his voice raised to combat the driving rhythmic thrum of a filling club.
874.	He turns to Garro and grips his shoulder. “I'll need a distraction in about five minutes. Can you make sure all eyes are on you?”
875.	Garro, leaning in sideways to hear, purses his lips with a little nod. “Yah, sure.” He looked about the club. It was early, but a good crowd was already present – the biggest problem he faced was finding some way to cause a distraction without bringing the entire building down.
876.	Having been trying to follow the shouted conversation, Mellie grabs Gerhardt's arm as he turns to go. “What are you doing?”
877.	“Don’t worry about it. Just keep things tight down here.” Giving an indicative chin nod at Garro. With that, he turns and begins to weave through the crowd, pulling Lynx along with him.
878.	As Gerhardt and Lynx made their way toward an exit to the back hallway that leads to Davie’s office, Garro stood in the midst of the writhing club, contemplating how to create the diversion Gerhardt had asked for. All about him, the music throbbed through the room, and the colourful lights played across the dancers' undulating bodies.
879.	He just needed to orchestrate something to draw the attention of the crowd. In the flickering lights, he spotted—almost directly above him—a large chandelier hanging from the ceiling, adorned with dozens of shimmering crystals. That will do.
880.	Meanwhile, Gerhardt and Lynx had wormed through the service corridors that the club's main room was embraced by and had reached the door that led up to the office.
881.	Gerhardt scanned the area, ensuring they weren't being watched; he leaned close to the electronic lock, his cybernetic arm unfolding to reveal the concealed automated lock-picking tools. He worked with precision and speed.
882.	Lynx was already making ready his deck, arranging a tangle of cables, booting up some of his go-to programs. Time was of the essence; they couldn't afford to leave any digital footprints. He connected his implanted agent, wrist bracer, and deck, turning them into a unified hacking machine.
883.	His eyes flicked through the data streams, searching for internal security broadcasts or emanations from the club's built-in protective or deterrent hardware.
884.	A soft click echoed in the corridor, barely audible over the thumping bass from the main room—Gerhardt had successfully unlocked the door. It swung open as Lynx inserted the last connection cable into his deck.
885.	They climbed the dimly lit stairs and pushed open the plush leather-clad door to the office. The room was dimly lit, its interior an eclectic mix of antique furniture and modern technology – And devoid of anything even remotely Davie-shaped.
886.	Back on the dance floor below, Garro had chosen his moment. As the bass dropped and the crowd reached a fever pitch, he swung upward with a borrowed chair—launching it. Faces were already turning as it struck the ceiling above.
887.	The chandelier trembled momentarily—and then, all at once, the connecting wires detached, twanging and snapping away from their anchors.
888.	Crystals shattered and showered down like sparkling rain, drawing gasps and cheers from the crowd. Lights flickered, plunging the vicinity into brief darkness. Screams and scattering bodies filled the space, all eyes captivated by the unexpected display.
889.	Taking advantage of the chaos, Garro made his way to the bar, dodging panicked patrons. He bellowed something unintelligible, further fuelling the disarray. In his haste, he knocked a scrawny man to the floor. The man scrambled back up and, without thinking, swung a fist into Garro’s lower back.
890.	There was, for that pin-point moment, a realisation that he’d made a poor choice as Garro turned slowly around, standing a clear head above him.
891.	Garro's retaliatory punch sent him skidding across the dance floor, knocked out cold and bleeding from a broken nose.
892.	The club's bouncers quickly responded, surging through the crowd that now tussled and shrieked about the erupting furore, clawing their way toward the assault.
893.	Previous partygoers were now quickly becoming a surge of screaming bystanders. The security honed in on the source of the uproar: Garro—standing head and shoulders above the panicked patrons.
894.	Upstairs in the office, Gerhardt and Lynx felt the press of each ticking second. Gerhardt nearly closed the door but left it ajar just enough to keep an eye on the stairwell. He readied his smart-linked pistol. " Lynx, move it. Clock's ticking," he said urgently.
895.	Lynx nodded, beads of sweat forming a glistening sheen on his face. Plugging his prepped deck into various ports around the room, streams of code filled the holographic displays and screens. His fingers danced across the virtual keyboard as he thrust into a virtualised maze of encrypted files.
896.	Reality fell away as Lynx allowed his consciousness to seep into the digital connections. It had been a while since his last deep dive, but the sensation welcomed him like an embrace from an old friend.
897.	Back on the dance floor, the situation escalated. Four bouncers had zeroed in on Garro. Their posture and expressions left no room for interpretation: they meant business.
898.	Despite being outnumbered, Garro held his ground, jutting his shoulders and bringing up his fists in anticipation.
899.	Two bouncers lunged at him simultaneously. The collision was like two freight cars crashing together. They wrestled at the edge of the dance floor, sending shattered crystals and spilt drinks scattering as they vied for control.
900.	A third bouncer, smaller than the others, pulled a taser from his jacket. The blue-white sparks flared in the colourful chaos of the club lights.
901.	A fourth was rapidly closing in when Mellie, sensing the escalating violence, swiftly drew her machine-pistol from its concealed holster. She didn’t fire, but the sight of the weapon alone froze the bouncers in their tracks.
902.	"Back off!" she yelled, her voice cutting through the pandemonium.
903.	Upstairs, Gerhardt sensed the commotion. He couldn't hear it through the soundproof glass, but he could feel it – His palm pressed against the wall, he picked up on the energetic thuds and shifts. It was as if he could almost visualize Garro wrestling with one bouncer clinging to his back while grappling with another, trying to restrain his arm.
904.	Listening to vibrations was a talent, and doing so to events a room away and a floor below was an art.
905.	His eyes darted to Lynx, sitting cross-legged, slumped against Davie's desk. For a horrifying moment, he looked like a corpse. The quick realisation dawned that the younger man had simply slipped his consciousness into a different world.
 
906.	Inside Lynx’s digitally entwined mind, he was bouncing three different automated programs in unison, scratching at a particularly difficult layer of encryption.
907.	In this virtual landscape, everything moved at the speed of thought. And in one of those fleeting instants, Lynx had an unsettling realization: he was up against Stepper's code. Though they'd never met, Lynx had a professional respect for Stepper. As Davie's go-to Netrunner, Stepper was responsible for the security measures behind anything Davie touched. If the complexity of this code-gate was any indication, Stepper lived up to their reputation.
908.	The virtual environment Lynx worked in was of his own making. Each section of code displayed with different iconography that he could, at a whim, pick up and re-shape. But, the barrier he now faced was refusing all of his best techniques.
909.	Even though the passage of time was irrelevant to someone as deep as he was now, seemingly infinite things happening concurrently in the space of one real second, He knew his friends were in danger, and he needed to act fast.
910.	In a desperate move, he repurposed one of his existing programs, stripping it down and reshaping it into a self-destructive worm. It was a costly piece of software to sacrifice, but it would be worth it if it got the job done and allowed him to rejoin the real world—meat-space, as he called it. With a flick of his virtual hand, he sent the worm crashing into the stubborn barrier.
 
911.	Garro's elbow swung back, dislodging the bouncer clinging to him. Mellie kept the two remaining bouncers at bay, brandishing her machine pistol as she scanned the club. It had emptied, leaving only Garro, still caught in his struggle, as its last remaining patron.
912.	With one arm finally free, Garro unleashed a massive fist into the chest of a bouncer, who staggered back, gasping for air. The other bouncer, probably cybernetically enhanced to hold his own against someone of Garro's size, tried to headbutt him but only succeeded in bloodying his own nose. However, he maintained his vice-like grip on Garro's arm.
913.	“Gee, we need to leave. There’s going to be badges all over this place!” Mellie yelled, trying to make herself heard over the pounding music and tousle of bodies. She kept her eyes trained on the remaining bouncers, her finger twitching near the trigger of her gun.
914.	Upstairs in the office, Lynx blinked, his consciousness lurching back into the room around him. “I got it,” he rasped, his eyes refocusing.
915.	Gerhardt snapped his attention to Lynx. “Good, now let’s get the hell out before this gets messy. Grab your shit,” he barked, gesturing with his oversized pistol towards Lynx's tangled mess of wires.
916.	Mellie had had enough. “Back off, I mean it!” she screamed, aiming her gun upwards and letting loose a burst of bullets. 
917.	Quickly, she redirected the gun back at the bouncers, one of whom was helping his wounded comrade while the other held a taser at the ready. “Garro, NOW!” she bellowed, urgency lacing her voice.
918.	A soft hiss emitted from Garro's metallic spine.
919.	In one quick motion, he lifted the remaining bouncer and hurled him toward his companions.
920.	He glanced at his ripped shirt and grimaced in disappointment. Pointing urgently at the exit, he hoped Mellie caught his gesture. Not waiting for a response, he broke into a rapid jog, each of his strides devouring the distance to the door.
921.	Mellie hadn't caught Garro's indication, but the sight of another bouncer flying sideways through the air was cue enough.
922.	She pivoted and bolted, spotting Garro ahead of her. She pushed her legs to go faster, her mind racing. I hope those idiots got whatever they were after.
923.	In a dimly lit alleyway, behind a popular club, somewhere in the Skrim, two figures clatter down a fire escape as distant sounds of sirens approach.
Chapter Fifteen:	Breadcrumbs.
924.	The crew huddled in Mellie's dimly-lit apartment, lit only by the flickering screens of Lynx's deck and the vid screen that silently displayed fluctuations of adverts. Lynx had extracted a significant amount of data from Davie's office, but decoding it was proving to be a daunting endeavour. 
925.	Lines of code and indecipherable symbols danced across the screens of his deck. Bathed in an eerie blue glow, Lynx twitched occasionally. He had chosen once again to jack in, allowing his mind direct unhindered access to the digital rather than test his metal using the manual inputs of his deck. His body lay unconscious on Mellie's couch, a tangle of familiar wires connecting him to his nearby deck.
926.	The others sat idly, awaiting his return. They shared a pack of protein bars and finished off the last of Mellie's light beer, her meagre food stores now depleted. Gerhardt had volunteered to keep watch and stationed himself outside Mellie's door.
927.	It promised to be another restless night. After their experience in the penthouse, they spent every second in tense anticipation. It felt as if the weight of the entire building, penthouse and all, was pulling them deeper into a quagmire of uncertainty.
928.	Nearly an hour had passed as they sat watching Lynx's occasional twitch, unable to truly comprehend what he was experiencing.
 
929.	The virtual space Lynx's consciousness now inhabited was a mess of recursive Mandelbrot zones—impossible landscapes that shifted and rearranged themselves responsively. Akin to trying to navigate a castle made of sand, where every move he made caused the walls to crumble and reshape, it was the data structure that Davie had secured all his information within—a digital ziggurat of immense scale. Lynx shifted within this space like water moving to fill cracks, trying to feel the path of least resistance.
930.	It was a marvel of coding. Lynx was in awe of what he had been fighting against for what felt like an eternity. However, he knew he had to decrypt the data, or he and the people waiting outside this digital realm would end up dead.
931.	Shifting his efforts to a more archaic approach required great mental strain.
932.	Now, he was swimming among raw code.
933.	He could see the writing and shifting bits; he could even taste the flow of memory chunks. It was laborious and far harder than what he was used to—this was the realm of Netrunners.
934.	But – bit by bit, he was undoing the defensive code. He made some errors and perhaps accidentally deleted some parts of the database, but he was progressing. The challenge was unique and demanded everything from him.
935.	When the final code section finally gave way, a rush of endorphins flooded his real body, making it flinch slightly.
936.	There it was: unshielded, open, and ready to be explored. Everything Davie had ever noted, every call he made, the flow of money, and the diaries of information were all at Lynx's virtual fingertips.
937.	As tempting as it was to explore, Lynx focused. He needed to know more about the jobs they'd been on, especially about the Penthouse.
938.	Using simple sorting algorithms, he filtered away everything unessential, allowing the comfort of his normal visual working space to return, drawing back from the rough raw code he’d sunk into.
939.	It was easier here to manipulate the data he was now racing through. Even his well-trained mind couldn't keep up with the computer's speed. Information flooded him like a tidal wave, forcing him to recenter himself and reset the system to make sense of it all.
940.	He took mental notes of several parts of the data and reformatted them into files for later retrieval. But now he understood how Davie picked jobs; Stepper operated a series of bulletin boards on an open net—highly illegal and risky. Using shells of fake information, it was here that Davie selected jobs from whatever was posted.
941.	Digging deeper, Lynx discovered regular connections to several other networks. While most were easily identified as communication networks, Lynx used Davie's contact list to create a virtual map of individuals within the city with whom Davie frequently communicated.
942.	Lynx then focused on a chunk of data related to this information, filtering out anything irrelevant to the night of their data-pack theft.
943.	A surprisingly small set of data remained, pointing to just three individuals. One, based on the message exchanges, appeared to be a preferred sex worker of Davie's, leaving only two contacts that seemed relevant.
944.	His work done, he looked at the comical black hole above him that was his chosen sense of escaping from a deep dive. Instinctive meditative muscle memory forced a virtual rope ladder to unfurl, a symbolic gesture that began to stir his physical body back to consciousness.
 
945.	“ffffmmfff”, Lynx tried to speak, but a residual paralysis constrained his muscles. He had never been so deeply immersed for so long. Netrunners used Ice-baths and injected chemicals to maintain their unconscious bodies; he had no such luxury.
946.	Mellie, alerted by the noise, furrowed her brow. “What? Did you say something?”
947.	Managing only a feeble “Wwmmffff,” Lynx's limp hand brushed against his lips in the dim screen glow. He snorted, straining to draw breath, willing his body back to normalcy.
948.	“Whas he sayin’ ?“ Garro leaned over the back of the couch; one eye squinted shut as he rubbed it with a fist, fighting off fatigue.
949.	“Waamffrr.” Lynx’s finger pulled at his lower lip, urging his dry mouth to catch up.
950.	“Water?!” Mellie exclaimed, deciphering his intent. Realizing he must be parched after his prolonged inactivity, He’d been motionless for so long, and while he’d been working, we’d all eaten and rested – he must be starving, she worried to herself.
951.	Mellie dashed to the nearby bathroom, snatching an empty cup along the way. She quickly filled it with water and hurried back. Garro, lending a hand, gently propped Lynx into a semi-upright position as Mellie carefully brought the cup to his lips.
952.	Water trickled down his chin, but Lynx reflexively swallowed, clumsily guiding the cup himself to tip the cup such that he could force its content into his face.
953.	After draining the cup, he shook it vigorously. “More!” His voice grated coarse and shallow.
954.	Mellie rushed to refill the cup, returning to find Lynx eagerly awaiting. “Did you find anything?” she asked as he gulped down the second mugful.
955.	Sputtering his last gulp, and between heavy laboured breaths, Lynx replied, “Absolute nightmare in there, almost didn’t get in. But I got some names.”
956.	He coughed against his hyperventilation, “Gilhan Novaris and Zoe Lansor”, he announced, shaking the empty cup again and exchanging a glance with Mellie. “Got their deets on the deck.”
957.	The door opened, and Gerhardt peered in, noting Lynx's revived state and the flurry of activity around him. “So?”
958.	“Shut the door”, Mellie barked.
959.	Lynx nodded at Gerhardt, a faint smile of triumph on his lips. "Yeah, got it."
960.	A ripple of cautious relief stirred among them as they pinned a hope against the names. If they could find what the data-pack was, or where it had gone, it could very well save their lives.
Chapter Sixteen:	Where The Wind Blows.
961.	Their initial inquiries led them to Gilhan Novaris—an obscure two-bit media dev. A brief visit—and an even briefer questioning facilitated by Garro's mere presence—quickly revealed just how minuscule a person Gilhan was. He fed Davie breaking stories, giving him the jump on any opportunities before anyone else in Akureyri got wind of them.
962.	The man's anxious demeanour and lack of knowledge made it abundantly clear that he was clueless about the penthouse. However, his interest piqued when Gerhardt mentioned a break-in at a high-end Corpo residence. They left him hungry.
963.	The second name on their list proved elusive, throwing the team into a standstill that, in light of Gilhan’s obsolescence, brought pause to hope and crashed against their collective nerves. Time seemed to stretch, each ticking second a reminder of their frustrating impasse.
964.	Lynx had insisted on leaving them, preferring solitude to pull at the frayed edges of information he had.
965.	He knew too well their troubles rested on his results. He did not enjoy being the focus of attention and needed the space to think properly.
966.	Mellie paced her apartment, her eyes shifting between the clock and others that had stayed with an absence of practical use to whatever Lynx was now doing.
967.	Her compassion was a steadying force for the team, but even her patience had its limits. Come on, Lynx, crack it already, she thought, clenching her fists. We're all depending on you. With a resigned sigh, she grabbed her jacket. “I'm going out to grab some food. Anyone want anything specific?”
968.	Gerhardt stood by the window, his gaze distant, lost in the cityscape yet not truly seeing it. His eyes, while fixed on the world outside, betrayed no hint of connection to it. In his mind, thoughts raced like a relentless storm – calculating, coldly dissecting each scenario. If Lynx can't get through, what's our next move? A break-in at a data centre? Bribery? Theft? Each possibility was weighed and measured with a dispassionate precision, his expression unchanging, as if the tumult of his thoughts were happening to someone else, somewhere far away. He failed to answer Mellie’s question.
969.	On the floor, Garro was engrossed in disassembling Mellie's machine gun. Each part was being cleaned with meticulous care, deep memories of a life long ago driving his actions. A clean gun is a reliable gun. A reliable gun keeps Mellie safe, he thought, comforted by the familiarity of the action.
970.	His focus shifted at her offer to get food, and his eyes brightened with a momentary distraction from the busywork. He paused—a gun part in hand—using it absentmindedly to scratch his stubbled chin.  “Oh, yeah, grab some SynthChips, the Wasabi ones. Thanks, Mellie.”
971.	As dusk settled over the city, Lynx finally returned.
972.	His tired face broke into a smile at the sight of the fresh food that Mellie had gathered. “I've got something concrete,” he announced, slicing through the room's tension as he stepped in.
973.	Mellie exhaled deeply, a wave of relief washing over her. Gerhardt locked eyes with Lynx, his nod conveying tacit acknowledgement of the breakthrough. Garro clicked the machine gun back together, a satisfied smile on his face.
 
974.	Flanked by Mellie and Garro, Lynx walked along the wide pavements of South-Central. The area was a regular haunt for those seeking shopping or more refined social spaces, close enough to the Skrim to attract a diverse crowd.
975.	“I had to trace the message nodes,” Lynx began. “I spent the afternoon in a café in Central. You wouldn't believe how many badges are around there. Luckily, I wasn't spotted. After hacking into the node, I found some Corpo dockets. Her name doesn't show up anywhere else.”
976.	Garro listened as he sipped a nutrient shake, giving the impression of paying attention even though the details seemed to slip right past him.
977.	Mellie brushed a hand through her lengthy hair, visibly irritated. “So, she’s a Corpo, seems about right for all this shit, and you have her address?”
978.	Lynx grimaced apologetically. “No, but I have the details of the pickup node where the docket was filed. There must be a bank or ATM nearby, and if so, there'll be cameras or logs I can access. The docket seems to be a regular payment– the packet size was nearly identical each time.”
979.	“Okay, so if we go there, you can find her?” Mellie had dropped to a deeper register, her annoyance evident.
980.	Lynx offered a wide-eyed, rapid, affirming nod.
981.	Turning decisively, Mellie commanded, “Garro, find wherever Gerhardt has gone and get him back here.” She softened her tone, adding, “And let’s hope this isn’t another dead end.”
982.	Tossing his empty shake cup into the gutter—oblivious that it was the only piece of litter in sight, a telling sign of the cultural divide between the Skrim and this cleaner neighbourhood—Garro pivoted and retraced their steps.
 
983.	The grey and glowing interior of the transport wait room buzzed with idle chatter. Commuters engaged in small talk while the walls flashed advertisements for housing, new cars, and the latest exercise machines.
984.	Slumped against a wall in the corner farthest from the boarding doors, a figure in a dark coat sat in quiet contemplation—the soft light dappled and fused on his face and cybernetic implants.
985.	Exhaustion dragged at Gerhardt, seeping into every part of his body—even the tiny pneumatics and mechanical servos of his augmented arm seemed to ache. He hadn't slept well since their return visit to the penthouse, and every night since had been consumed by urgent matters.
986.	Drawing a deep breath, he blinked away the heaviness in his eyes, his attention having slipped from his task. Where are you, you shitbag, his closing thoughts on the dismissed matter. Although he had some experience working in cyberspace, his attempts felt like those of an amateur tinkerer trying to repair a fine antique watch; most of it was beyond his capability.
987.	Within his internal viewscreen, blinking sections of code truncated his real vision. Ushering away the overlay and refocusing on his surroundings, he took stock of the small yet bustling room.
988.	It was a typical waiting area for the city's cross-district monorails, and no one paid much attention to the stoic, distant-looking man in the long black coat. Everyone seemed wrapped in their own bubbles of ignorance.
989.	The frustrations of his failed hunt were bubbling. Still, even those were overshadowed by the looming anxiety of the multitude of people that were now hunting him: Cit-Sec, the Buzokuh, maybe even corporate solos—Just staying alive had become a draining endeavour. This new problem had been layered on top of it all. It was an amusement in all of his concern, knowing that just days earlier, he’d all but insulted a Cit-Sec captain to his face.
990.	The world felt topsy-turvey, and he was locked in a box with mad people. At least Davie’s pet - Lynx, had proven to be useful.
991.	Garro's entrance into the room was impossible to ignore. His broad frame filled the doorway, and his deep voice reverberated through the space. “Hey Jerry, Mellie says we need ya.”
992.	A dozen faces turned, each person wondering who the giant was addressing. Those nearest took a step back.
993.	Suppressing an internal groan and thinking to himself, why must everyone give me stupid fucking nicknames?—Gerhardt stood. “Sure, I’m coming”, he replied.
994.	A collective muted sigh of relief rippled through the room as the commuters realized the ragged giant wasn't looking for them.
 
995.	Taking public transport slowed their journey to the city's upper northwestern side. The dense forests of towering blocks gradually thinned, giving way to more sparsely planted rows of low-rise apartments. They found themselves walking along a street so unremarkable that they'd already forgotten its existence.
996.	Cars sped past them from time to time. The grid-like streets here were narrower than the sprawling avenues of Central, yet less chaotic than the tangled maze that was the Skrim. Overhead, the late sun cast a warm, lazy glow on the day while dried litter skittered about on flurries of a breeze.
997.	Turning a corner, they came to an abrupt stop as a group. Ahead of them—some few hundred yards—the street flashed and bloomed with the silent but present lights of several Cit-Sec wagons. A small cluster of uniformed officers stood by one of the wagons, engaged in a discussion. Adding to the entourage were a pair of bipedal sentry droids stationed at the side of the street. Their authoritative black-red paint starkly contrasted with the green lawns and red-orange buildings around them.
998.	“That does not look good”, Gerhardt slowly injected into their collective pause.
999.	“Ahh, nuts” Lynx sagged. “I think that’s where we need to be.” He expelled a long-drawn sigh of his frustration.
1000.	“Well, we were told not to interfere with Cit-Sec, and I see no reason to put our faces in front of them right now. What's the play?” Gerhardt studied Mellie’s face; her eyes were narrowed, her lips pursed—deep in thought.
1001.	Glancing at Garro, Gerhardt noticed the big man seemed disinterested in the scene, seemingly content to bask in the warmth of the late-day sun.
1002.	“What was the address you had?” Mellie quickly asked Lynx, trying to keep pace with the muse of her forming ideas.
1003.	Lynx recited an address displayed on his digital arm bracer. Judging by the numbers on the nearby buildings, the address he provided appeared to be beyond the cordon.
1004.	Even before Lynx could finish speaking, Mellie was already halfway into her explanation. “You can just slip through and check the place out, right?”
1005.	“No way am I going anywhere near that,” Lynx said, jutting a finger and pointing down the street.
1006.	Mellie, fully aware she was armed, didn't like her odds if they had scanners. And the idea of being unarmed made her uncomfortable. Her eyes briefly met Gerhardt's, but his expression was answer enough to a question she hadn't even voiced.
1007.	Noticing Gerhardt's quiet gaze, Mellie followed it to Garro. That’s not a terrible idea, she inwardly insisted.
1008.	“Hey, Gee, you think you can reccy the situation there?” Without a word, he shrugged, a nonchalant carefree agreement.
1009.	“Okay, we’ll wait for Garro to check the place out—And…” she turned to look up and catch Garro’s attention, “this time, don't punch anyone.”
1010.	“Well, okay then,” Garro responded, his confirmation sounding more like social courtesy than an actual commitment.
1011.	She continued, “Find out if the building is totally locked down, get eyes on the apartment if you can, and just come right back.” Mellie’s instructions were concise, hoping to avoid complications.
1012.	Garro left them, striding toward the cordoned off by wagons and temporary barriers that emitted flickering holographic warnings. The words [DO NOT CROSS] floated, glowing in the air in front of him.
1013.	As he moved closer, two dark-uniformed officers mirrored his approach, one extending his arm palm-out, signalling him to stop.
1014.	Garro ignored them but paused before the barrier.
1015.	“What’s your business here, sir?” The officer closest to him spoke dryly, his voice partially muffled by the half-visor covering his face.
1016.	Caught off guard by the direct question, Garro hesitated. Mellie hadn't prepared him for this. For a moment, he stood there ponderously, letting his brain squash through the mire of drugs awash in his body.
1017.	“Came to see me maw,” he blurted out.
1018.	The bluff was a rudimentary reaction, as basic as it could get, and Garro sounded almost astonished as he heard himself say it. The two visors exchanged a moment, "Wait here," said the one who had been speaking before retreating, tapping a hand to the side of his helmet and uttering something indistinguishable to nobody.
1019.	A moment passed—Garro blinked, looking at the sky—Huh, that was a bird, he pondered as a dark silhouette floated away past the horizon of the distant apartments.
1020.	“You got a name?” the visor had asked. Garro returned his attention to the man.
1021.	“Gar—” He caught himself and adjusted his posture to emphasize his somewhat unnatural height. “S–F–S Piotr Garonosovietch.” The words were delivered with practised staccato.
1022.	There was an observable pause as the two visors stood facing him. The one that had touched his helmet did so again.
1023.	“This building is on crime scene lockdown, sir—” the officer began.
1024.	Garro interrupted the talking mouth below the dark visor, “I just wanna check in on my ma’. Can't you just let me through?” He was struggling now to retain his thoughts; the ever-present drugs were winning; clouds of uncertainty threatened to trip his voice. He strained to recall the address Lynx had given him – Thirty-four bee.
1025.	Another pause ensued, punctuated by the flashing neon blues of the patrol wagons' lights. Garro stared at his illuminated reflection in the officers' dark visors.
1026.	“Fine, but no screwing about, you go wherever it is you’re going. If you cause any trouble, I’ll book you for interference.” The indifference of the officer blistered on every word; he had outwardly emitted an absence of desire to be wasting his day ushering people about. He waved a thick-gloved hand at the building and took a step back.
1027.	Garro let his eyes blink from one dead-faced visor to the other, both seemingly eager for him to leave. He walked toward the indicated building. As he got closer, the two droids—each with a heavy gun gripped in their metallic, skeletal, clamped fingers—tracked his movements. Their boxy heads rotated in unsettling unison to his movement.
1028.	Their stillness was unnerving; statues he could feel staring at him through a multitude of tiny lenses. He stepped into the double doors into the tight lobby of the building.
1029.	Inside, the muted colours and modest materials were rhythmically emblazoned blue. Light from a few suspended ceiling fixtures cast a warm glow on faked wood veneer wall panels, making the space feel more intimate than it was. 
1030.	Faux-leather armchairs were arranged around low coffee tables in small clusters, creating pseudo-private spaces for casual conversations. A reception desk sat unoccupied at one side, its surface clean except for a stack of outdated magazines and a potted snake plant that had seen better days.
1031.	Ahead, a broad staircase dominated the centre of the hall; a digital directory was mounted on the wall nearby, its screen flickering intermittently. The carpet underfoot was a nondescript geometric pattern designed to hide stains but did little to elevate the room's aesthetic. 
1032.	The air smelled faintly of cleaning products, overlaid with the more organic scents of the building's inhabitants—cooked food, perfume, and the occasional whiff of pet odours. “Thirty-four bee, third floor.” he reminded himself. Looking at the central stairwell, he saw another droid at the top.
1033.	Garro took a moment to centre himself; realising adrenaline had set his entire body into a tense coil. Thirty-four b, he repeated mentally. Striving for nonchalance—difficult given his imposing size—he passed the watchful droid. Its boxy head swivelled to track him, the dim lobby light glinting off its surface.
1034.	Upon reaching the third floor, he found the hallway deserted. Scattered trash blended into the stained carpet that ran its length. Ahead, the shattered remnants of a door and its frame stood out amid the grime.
1035.	Closing the distance to the wreckage, he observed that high-tensile security wire had been tacked across the gap where the door once stood. Amid the splinters and debris at his feet, a brass etched plate read [34 b]
